# HellForge mod SDK reference (game build 25480438)

For code mod authors who need to look something up: every call in the `HfApi` table, every game function and global mapped
by name, every component and settings table field, and every vanilla content constant, for game build 25480438. To
learn how to make a mod, follow the
guide in [modding/README.md](modding/README.md), starting with [your first mod](modding/first-mod.md), and keep this
page open beside it. Models, textures, effects, sounds and icons are covered in [Data mods](modding/data.md).

Search the page for the name you need (a call such as `set_live`, a struct such as `HfWeaponMagazine`, a constant
such as `HF_ITEM_...`), or use the contents below.

This page is generated by `HellForge.Cli sdk-headers` from `native/mod-sdk/hellforge_mod.h` (the API, as documented
there) and `mappings/` (functions, globals, fields and content); do not edit it by hand. To change it, edit those sources and regenerate
(docs/sdk-roadmap.md, "Mappings"; [Tools and sharing](modding/tools.md) has the command).

- [Components](#components): [HfProjectileWeapon](#hfprojectileweapon), [HfWeaponData](#hfweapondata), [HfWeaponMagazine](#hfweaponmagazine), [HfTag](#hftag), [HfMount](#hfmount), [HfBehavior](#hfbehavior), [HfLoadoutPackage](#hfloadoutpackage), [HfDeposit](#hfdeposit), [HfHealth](#hfhealth), [HfJumppack](#hfjumppack), [HfRecharge](#hfrecharge), [HfShield](#hfshield), [HfHellpodPayload](#hfhellpodpayload), [HfSensorEye](#hfsensoreye), [HfTurret](#hfturret), [HfWeaponWindUp](#hfweaponwindup), [HfBeamWeapon](#hfbeamweapon), [HfWeaponHeat](#hfweaponheat), [HfEagle](#hfeagle), [HfThrower](#hfthrower), [HfBombardment](#hfbombardment), [HfExplosive](#hfexplosive), [HfThrowable](#hfthrowable), [HfVehicleMotion](#hfvehiclemotion), [HfWeaponCharge](#hfweaponcharge), [HfWeaponReload](#hfweaponreload), [HfWeaponRounds](#hfweaponrounds), [HfArcWeapon](#hfarcweapon), [HfHiveMind](#hfhivemind), [HfOrbitalAbility](#hforbitalability), [HfRotation](#hfrotation), [HfDisplacement](#hfdisplacement), [HfSeekingMissile](#hfseekingmissile), [HfLoadoutEntry](#hfloadoutentry), [HfHellpodRack](#hfhellpodrack), [HfAvatar](#hfavatar), [HfInventory](#hfinventory), [HfMeleeWeapon](#hfmeleeweapon), [HfAbilityWeapon](#hfabilityweapon), [HfUnit](#hfunit), [HfSquadTransportFormation](#hfsquadtransportformation), [HfSquadTransport](#hfsquadtransport), [HfGore](#hfgore), [HfAiSpawner](#hfaispawner), [HfStratagemBlocker](#hfstratagemblocker), [HfBughole](#hfbughole), [HfEquipment](#hfequipment), [HfWieldable](#hfwieldable), [HfAttachable](#hfattachable), [HfWeaponCustomization](#hfweaponcustomization)
- [Settings tables](#settings-tables): [HfDifficultySettings](#hfdifficultysettings), [HfDamageInfo](#hfdamageinfo), [HfProjectileSettings](#hfprojectilesettings), [HfStratagemSettings](#hfstratagemsettings), [HfExplosionSettings](#hfexplosionsettings), [HfArcSettings](#hfarcsettings), [HfBeamSettings](#hfbeamsettings), [HfStatusEffectSettings](#hfstatuseffectsettings), [HfBoosterSettings](#hfboostersettings), [HfPassiveModifier](#hfpassivemodifier), [HfPassiveStat](#hfpassivestat), [HfMeleeHitVolume](#hfmeleehitvolume), [HfEnemyRoster](#hfenemyroster), [HfEnemyRosterLevel11](#hfenemyrosterlevel11), [HfEnemyComposition](#hfenemycomposition), [HfDemolitionTemplate](#hfdemolitiontemplate)

## API

The `HfApi` table a mod's `hellforge_mod_init(const HfApi* hf, HfMod* mod)` receives. Functions are only ever added at
the end, and a mod checks `hf->version` to know which ones the loader has. Each section lists what one SDK version
added, in table order; a version that added nothing to the table has no section. Each entry gives the member's
name, its type, and its comment from `hellforge_mod.h`. Where one comment covers several members, it is on the
first of them.

See also: [Logic at run time](modding/runtime.md) for events, actions and live edits; [Content](modding/content.md)
for changing and adding content; [Hooks and game memory](modding/hooks.md) for `hook`, `find_pattern`, `read` and
`write`; [Debugging](modding/debugging.md) for the mod's log and refusals.

### SDK 1

- `version`: `uint32_t`. HF_MOD_SDK_VERSION of the loader; functions are only ever added at the end.
- `size`: `uint32_t`. sizeof(HfApi) of the loader.
- `log`: `void (HfMod* mod, const char* format, ...)`. Logging to the mod's own log in the session folder (printf format).
- `build_id`: `const char* (void)`. The supported game build ("25480438") and the mod's own id and folder (UTF-8).
- `mod_id`: `const char* (HfMod* mod)`
- `mod_folder`: `const char* (HfMod* mod)`
- `on`: `int (HfMod* mod, HfEvent event, HfEventCallback callback, void* user)`. Calls back once when the event happens (at once if it already did). Returns 0 on success.
- `module_base`: `uintptr_t (const char* module, size_t* size)`. A loaded module's base address and image size, or 0 ("game.dll", "helldivers2.exe").
- `find_pattern`: `uintptr_t (const char* module, const char* pattern)`. The first match of an IDA-style byte pattern ("48 8B ?? 24 ?? E8") in the module's image, or 0. It reads the code as it is now: a function another mod or HellForge hooked starts with a jump. To check a function before hooking it, use code_matches (SDK 13), which reads its bytes from before any hook.
- `read`: `int (uintptr_t address, void* buffer, size_t size)`. Memory: 0 on success. write makes the bytes writable for the write, restores their protection and flushes the instruction cache, so it works for code and data.
- `write`: `int (uintptr_t address, const void* data, size_t size)`
- `write_u32_if`: `int (HfMod* mod, uintptr_t address, uint32_t expect, uint32_t value)`. Writes a u32 only if it holds `expect` (or already `value`): 0 written or already set, 1 left alone.
- `hook`: `int (HfMod* mod, uintptr_t target, void* detour, void** original)`. Detours the function at `target` to `detour`; *original receives a pointer that calls the next hook in the function's chain, or its own code. 0 on success. Hooks stay for the session. Since SDK 13 every hook, a mod's or HellForge's own, joins one chain per function, so several mods can hook the same function: hook_ex (SDK 13) says the order, and this is hook_ex with priority 0. Call the original through `*original` every time, never a copy of it: HellForge rewrites it when another hook joins the chain after this one. Code another overlay already redirected (its jump stub in allocated memory, where MinHook finds no room) is chained: the stub's address becomes the detour, the overlay's code the original.
- `network_field_type`: `int (const char* name, HfFieldType* type)`. Network field types by name (after HF_EVENT_NETWORK_READY). get: 0 and *type filled, or 1. set_bits widens an integer type, only if it is exactly `expect` (or already widened): 0 on success, 1 left alone.
- `set_network_field_bits`: `int (HfMod* mod, const char* name, HfFieldType expect, int32_t bits)`
- `component_record`: `uintptr_t (const char* component, uint64_t resource, uint32_t* stride)`. The address of an entity's record of a component ("DepositComponentData") after HF_EVENT_ENTITIES_READY, or 0 when the entity owns no such record, owns several, or shares it with another entity. *stride receives the record size. Resource ids are the 64-bit entity resource hashes HD2Runtime's catalogs list.

### SDK 2

- `add_weapon`: `uint32_t (HfMod* mod, const HfWeapon* weapon)`. Adds a new arsenal weapon (see HfWeapon). HellForge adds it to the game's entity data when that loads, to the armory, and to the backend's item catalogue and the player's inventory; it behaves exactly like its base except for what the mod changes in its own records. Call it from hellforge_mod_init. Returns the new weapon's loadout id, or 0 (the mod's log says why).
- `weapon_entity`: `uint64_t (uint32_t weapon)`. The entity resource of a weapon any mod added (its loadout id), for component_record; 0 if there is none.

### SDK 3

- `add_projectile`: `uint32_t (HfMod* mod, const HfProjectile* projectile)`. Adds a new projectile type (see HfProjectile). Call it from hellforge_mod_init. Returns its ProjectileType (351 and up), or 0 (the mod's log says why). 64 new types in all mods together (HellForge moves the game's list).
- `projectile_row`: `uintptr_t (uint32_t type)`. The 272-byte settings row of a projectile a mod added, after HF_EVENT_PROJECTILES_READY, or 0. Its +0 is the type and must stay so; the rest may be changed (HD2Runtime's research names the members).
- `add_damage`: `uint32_t (HfMod* mod, const HfDamage* damage)`. Adds a new damage type (see HfDamage). Call it from hellforge_mod_init, before the projectiles that deal it. Returns its id (650 and up), or 0 (the mod's log says why).
- `damage_row`: `uintptr_t (uint32_t id)`. The 76-byte DamageInfo row of a damage type a mod added, after HF_EVENT_PROJECTILES_READY, or 0: +0 id (must stay), +4 standard damage, +8 durable damage, +0x0C four armour penetration values, +0x1C on status effects.
- `resource_hash`: `uint64_t (const char* name)`. The game's 64-bit id of a resource name ("my_name/my_mod/bolt"): what the game's data and rows store.

### SDK 4

- `add_stratagem`: `uint32_t (HfMod* mod, const HfStratagem* stratagem)`. Adds a new stratagem (see HfStratagem). Call it from hellforge_mod_init. Returns its StratagemType (150 and up), or 0 (the mod's log says why). There is room for 1,024 new stratagems in all mods together.
- `stratagem_row`: `uintptr_t (uint32_t type)`. The 400-byte settings row of a stratagem a mod added, once the game has loaded its stratagems, or 0. +0 type and +4 stable id must stay; HD2Runtime's research names the rest (docs/internals.md "Stratagems").
- `add_entity`: `uint64_t (HfMod* mod, const HfEntity* entity)`. Adds a new entity (see HfEntity). Call it from hellforge_mod_init. Returns its resource id, or 0 (the mod's log says why). Entities and weapons share the game's entity map, about 2,187 more across all mods; HellForge sets no number of its own.
- `add_behaviour`: `uint32_t (HfMod* mod, const HfBehaviour* behaviour)`. Adds a new behaviour (see HfBehaviour). Call it from hellforge_mod_init. Returns its id (694 and up), or 0 (the mod's log says why). An entity given it runs its base instead if HellForge could not install it.

### SDK 5

- `add_difficulty`: `uint32_t (HfMod* mod, const HfDifficulty* difficulty)`. Adds difficulty level 11 (see HfDifficulty). Call it from hellforge_mod_init. Returns 11, or 0 (the mod's log says why). One mod can add it.
- `difficulty_row`: `uintptr_t (uint32_t level)`. The 0x330-byte settings row of a level (1 .. 11) once HellForge has moved the table, or 0. +0 unlock item and +4 level must stay (artifacts/difficulty11/FINDINGS.md names the members known).

### SDK 6

- `register_entity`: `HfRef (HfMod* mod, const HfEntityDef* entity)`. Registers a new entity from its definition block (see HfEntityDef), with the entities its mount carries and the behaviour it runs. Call it from hellforge_mod_init. Registering the same block again returns the same entity. Returns the entity, or 0 (the mod's log says why, with the source line of a field the loader refused).
- `copy_field`: `int (HfMod* mod, HfRef entity, HfRef from, const HfField* field)`. The calls behind definition blocks, for code: each queues a deferred edit, applied whenever the game loads its entity data (at once if it has). An entity a mod added gets a record of its own in the field's component when the call comes from hellforge_mod_init (later, it must own one already); a vanilla entity's record is changed only if no other entity shares it. Another mod (or a stats mod) changing the same field is reported in both mods' logs and modloader.log; the later edit's value stays. 0 queued, 1 refused (the mod's log says why). Prefer the hf_copy, hf_set, hf_tag and hf_mount macros below.
- `set_field`: `int (HfMod* mod, HfRef entity, const HfField* field, const HfValue* value)`
- `set_tag`: `int (HfMod* mod, HfRef entity, uint64_t tag, int present)`
- `mount`: `int (HfMod* mod, HfRef entity, uint32_t slot, HfRef carried)`
- `record`: `void* (HfMod* mod, HfRef entity, const char* component, uint32_t layout, uint32_t size)`. An entity's own record of a component after HF_EVENT_ENTITIES_READY, checked against the layout the mod was built with, or NULL (the mod's log says why). Use hf_record.
- `find`: `uint64_t (uint32_t kind, const char* name)`. Content by name, or 0: vanilla content by its catalog name (find(HF_KIND_ENTITY, "Pelican Transport"); the hellforge_content.h constants have the same ids), or content a mod added by its identity ("skyeshade.pelican-support:pelican_gatling", or the full name it was added under). For names known only at run time (a config file, another mod's content); code that knows the content uses the constants.
- `edit_entity`: `int (HfMod* mod, const HfEditDef* edit)`. Changes an existing entity from a definition block (see HfEditDef). 0 queued, 1 refused (the mod's log says why, with the source line of a field the loader refused).
- `set_live`: `int (HfMod* mod, HfRef target, const HfField* field, const HfValue* value, uint32_t flags)`. Changes a field in the running game (live editing): the same deferred edit as set_field, applied at once and kept for the rest of the session (renewed if the game reloads its data), with the same checks and conflict reports. The target is found by identity every time, never by address: an entity by its resource id (a vanilla one or one a mod added), or a settings table row by its id (HfProjectileSettings: a projectile type; HfDamageInfo: a damage id; HfStratagemSettings: a stratagem type; HfDifficultySettings: a level; the explosion, arc, beam and status tables: their row ids). The mod's next live edit of the same field replaces this one; value NULL undoes it, putting back the value from before it. A record other entities share is changed only with HF_LIVE_SHARED (it changes all of them, as a stats file does). Returns when the change applies (HF_APPLIES_...), or 0 refused (the mod's log says why). Nothing is saved: a live editor saves into a stats file (hellforge-stats.json).
- `get_live`: `int (HfMod* mod, HfRef target, const HfField* field, HfLiveValue* out)`. Reads a field of the running game, found as set_live finds it (a shared record too): its value now and its base. Returns when a change to it applies (HF_APPLIES_...), or 0 when it cannot be read (no such record, or its table is not loaded yet).
- `set_passives`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint32_t armor, uint32_t second)`. Overrides the local player's armor passive and second passive (the helmet's slot), solo only: the game's attribute reader takes the armor passive's bonuses first, then the second's that the armor lacks. The override is kept (written again after a kit change) until both are HF_PASSIVE_KIT; with other players in, the kits' values return and it waits. Refused per slot, and said in get_passives' status: a passive whose effect needs a package (INTEGRATED EXPLOSIVES, ADRENO-DEFIBRILLATOR) unless the armor gives it, INTEGRATED EXPLOSIVES as the second. Returns 1 held (or released), 0 for an id the game has no passive for.
- `get_passives`: `int (HfMod* mod, HfPassives* out)`. The local player's passives now; returns 1 when the record is readable (the status says why not).
- `on_game_thread`: `int (HfMod* mod, HfTickCallback callback, void* user)`. Calls back every game update on the game thread (the thread the game's own engine calls come from: sounds, spawns, package requests), before the game's update. Keep it short; it runs every frame. Returns 1, or 0 when the game thread's update could not be hooked (modloader.log says why). Check hf->size before calling.
- `load_package`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint64_t package)`. Loads a package (by its resource name hash, as mappings/references.json lists them, or since SDK 17 a data spec's own "package" of any mod of the session) and holds it for the session, through the game's own package system on the game thread: HF_PACKAGE_RESIDENT once the engine has all of it, HF_PACKAGE_LOADING until then (call it again to see), 0 refused or failed (neither kind, HellForge's 64 held already, or not loaded within 90 seconds; the mod's log says why). Loading is per client: other players load their own. A reference set_live or a stats file writes (a donor projectile, an explosion) loads its packages itself.
- `last_log`: `const char* (HfMod* mod)`. The mod's last log line (say, why the call that just returned 0 was refused), and the session's logs folder (UTF-8: modloader.log and every mod-<id>.log), or "". Check hf->size before calling.
- `logs_folder`: `const char* (HfMod* mod)`
- `get_stat`: `int (HfMod* mod, const HfStat* stat, HfLiveValue* out)`. A stats field's value now and its base (HfLiveValue's), for any field the session's stats snapshot reads, the fields get_live cannot (entity deltas, say). 1 when read, 0 not (not in the snapshot, or not read yet).
- `session`: `void (HfMod* mod, HfSession* out)`. The session's co-op role (HfSession), as the session's backend tells the loader.

### SDK 7

- `subscribe`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint32_t kind, HfListener listener, void* user, int32_t priority)`. Listens to one kind of game event (HF_ON_..., see HfEventInfo), on the game thread, highest priority first (equal priorities in subscription order). Call it from hellforge_mod_init. Returns 1, or 0 (the mod's log says why: an unknown kind, or the game thread not hooked). A kind's source is hooked once a mod listens to it; one that does not read as reviewed on this game build delivers nothing, and modloader.log says so.
- `game_state`: `uint32_t (HfMod* mod)`. The game's state now (HF_STATE_...), or HF_STATE_NONE before the game has one.
- `content`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint32_t index, HfContent* out)`. The index-th piece of content mods added (stratagems, entities, projectiles, damage, then arsenal weapons once registered; see HfContent): 1 with *out filled, 0 past the last. Complete once every mod's hellforge_mod_init has run (from HF_EVENT_GAME_LOADED); the weapons from HF_EVENT_ENTITIES_READY.
- `add_ability`: `uint32_t (HfMod* mod, const HfAbility* ability)`. Adds a new ability (see HfAbility). Call it from hellforge_mod_init. Returns its id (2868 and up), or 0 (the mod's log says why). An entity naming it never sets it off if HellForge could not install it (modloader.log).
- `add_stratagem_category`: `uint32_t (HfMod* mod, const HfStratagemCategory* category)`. SDK 8: returns a selection-category handle, or 0 with a logged reason. Re-registering an identical fully qualified definition returns the same handle; a conflicting title/order is refused.

### SDK 9

- `set_stat`: `int (HfMod* mod, const HfStat* stat, const HfValue* value, uint32_t flags)`. Changes a stats field set_live cannot reach (an entity delta: an attachment's or a default magazine's stats; a status multiplier), the fields get_stat reads, live, in the place a stats file writes it at boot. value NULL: back to its value from before this session's live change, or with HF_LIVE_BASE to its base (get_stat's). Returns HF_APPLIES_RESPAWN (a weapon takes an attachment's value when it is next built, expected) or 0 (the mod's log says why). Check hf->size before calling.
- `copy_row`: `int (HfMod* mod, const char* table, uint64_t row, uint64_t from, uint32_t offset, uint32_t size)`. A settings row's bytes (offset, size) replaced live by another row's of the same table, for the rows HellForge's catalogue names (mappings/references.json "rowCopies": the G-109 Urchin's pulse explosion), from the donors listed there, once the donor's packages are resident (it waits for them). from 0 puts the row's own bytes back. Returns HF_APPLIES_NOW or 0 (the mod's log says why). Check hf->size before calling.

### SDK 10

- `mods`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint32_t index, HfModInfo* out)`. The index-th mod of the session (HfModInfo): the code and stats mods in load order, then the Lua mods as they register through the Lua bridge (docs/lua-bridge.md). 1 with *out filled, 0 past the last. Check hf->size.

### SDK 11: actions (HF_ACT_...; check hf->size before calling)

- `heal`: `int (HfMod* mod, float amount)`. Heals the local Helldiver by `amount` health (each limb by the same share), up to its maximum.
- `heal_limb`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint32_t limb)`. Heals one of the local Helldiver's limbs (HF_LIMB_...) in full, and mends it.
- `injure`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint32_t limb, int32_t damage)`. Damages one of the local Helldiver's limbs (1 .. its maximum: head 85, chest 60, arms 35, legs 45); it may down or kill the Helldiver, as the game's own hit would.
- `limb_state`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint32_t entity, uint32_t limb, HfLimbState* out)`. A Helldiver's limb now: its health, maximum and whether it is injured (any Helldiver this machine has).
- `apply_status`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint32_t entity, uint32_t status, float buildup)`. Applies a status (HF_STATUS_...) to an entity: `buildup` 0 .. 1000 (100: as a full hit); host.
- `add_velocity`: `int (HfMod* mod, const float delta[3])`. Adds to the local Helldiver's velocity (m/s, world space, Z up; each part at most 25, the result at most 50). Walking resets sideways speed every update: an upward push is the dependable one.
- `resupply_from_pack`: `int (HfMod* mod)`. The local Helldiver uses its B-1 Supply Pack on itself, as the game's own input does (one supply).
- `spawn_explosion`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint32_t type, const float position[3])`. An explosion (its ExplosionSettings row) at a position, credited to this machine's player; host. Loads the explosion's packages first (HF_ACT_WAITING until they are in).
- `spawn_projectile`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint32_t type, const float position[3], const float direction[3])`. A projectile (its ProjectileSettings type) fired from a position along a direction, as the local Helldiver's; host. Loads its packages first.
- `credit_to_player`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint32_t entity)`. The kills of an entity (a turret, a vehicle a mod spawned) credited to its owner, this machine's player; solo host.
- `freeze_mission_timer`: `int (HfMod* mod, int frozen)`. Pauses (1) or resumes (0) the mission timer; host. A new mission starts it again.
- `spawn_pelican`: `int (HfMod* mod, const HfPelicanCall* call, uint32_t* entity)`. Calls in a transport Pelican (HfPelicanCall); *entity its entity. Host.
- `modify_shots`: `uint32_t (HfMod* mod, const HfShotRule* rule)`. Shot rules: an id (1 and up), or 0 refused. stop_shots ends one (later shots fly as the game makes them). One rule of each kind per projectile type.
- `homing`: `uint32_t (HfMod* mod, const HfHoming* rule)`
- `stop_shots`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint32_t id)`

### SDK 12

- `notice`: `void (HfMod* mod, HfNotice* out)`. HellForge's latest message for the player (HfNotice), as the session's backend posts it. Check hf->size.

### SDK 13

- `hook_ex`: `int (HfMod* mod, uintptr_t target, void* detour, void** original, int32_t priority)`. hook with an order: a function's hooks run first to last, each calling the next through its original. Mods' hooks run first, the highest `priority` first; equal priorities by load order, the mod higher on the Mods page first (a mod's own later hook on the same function before its earlier one). HellForge's own hooks (its events, the registry, the game thread) run last, nearest the game's code. The mod's log and modloader.log name each hook with its place in the chain, a function hooked by more than one mod or by HellForge too is logged as a shared hook with every owner in order, and the session folder's hooks.tsv lists every chain. Refused (1, the mod's log says why): the same detour on the same function twice, more than 16 hooks on one function or 1024 hooked functions in all. Check hf->size before calling.
- `code_matches`: `int (uintptr_t address, const char* pattern)`. 1 when the bytes at `address`, as they were before any hook HellForge placed (its own or a mod's), match the IDA-style pattern ("48 8B ?? 24"); 0 otherwise or when they cannot be read. The check to make before hooking a function: another mod that hooked it first does not fail it, where find_pattern and read would (they see the hook's jump). Only hooks are seen through: bytes a mod changed with write, or that HellForge rewrote, read as they are now. Check hf->size before calling.
- `read_code`: `int (uintptr_t address, void* buffer, size_t size)`. Like read, with the bytes as they were before any hook HellForge placed (as code_matches reads them). 0 on success.

### SDK 14: mod sync (HfSyncState; check hf->size before calling)

- `sync_state`: `int (HfMod* mod, HfSyncState* out)`. The current offer: 1 with *out filled (at most out->size bytes), 0 none (solo, or no backend connected).
- `sync_mod`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint32_t index, HfSyncMod* out)`. The index-th mod of the current offer: 1 with *out filled (at most out->size bytes), 0 past the last. Its flags: HF_SYNC_FLAG_STRANGER (warn harder before HF_SYNC_TRUST) and HF_SYNC_FLAG_SANDBOX_ONLY (a sandboxed game: a trust answered here counts only in sandboxed sessions, since any code in the game can call sync_answer; say so).
- `sync_answer`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint32_t version, uint32_t answer, const char* mod_id)`. Sends the player's answer (HF_SYNC_ACCEPT ...) to the offer of that version; mod_id names the mod for HF_SYNC_BLOCK, HF_SYNC_TRUST and HF_SYNC_OPEN (NULL otherwise). 1 sent; 0 not (another offer came since, or no backend connected).

### SDK 15

- `stats_source`: `uint32_t (HfMod* mod)`. Whose stats the lobby plays with: HF_STATS_HOST or HF_STATS_OWN (see HF_STATS_OWN). Check hf->size.

### SDK 16: the lobby browser (HfBrowseState; check hf->size before calling)

- `browse_state`: `int (HfMod* mod, HfBrowseState* out)`. The list as the backend last sent it: 1 with *out filled (at most out->size bytes), 0 when no backend is connected (*out is filled all the same: state HF_BROWSE_NONE, and the terminal count).
- `browse_lobby`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint32_t index, HfBrowseLobby* out)`. The index-th lobby of the list: 1 with *out filled (at most out->size bytes), 0 past the last.
- `browse_ask`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint32_t ask, const char* lobby_id)`. Asks the backend for a new list (HF_BROWSE_REFRESH, lobby_id NULL) or to join lobby_id (HF_BROWSE_JOIN). 1 sent; 0 not (no backend connected, or a malformed ask). The answer arrives through browse_state. HF_BROWSE_QUICK joins the best lobby, and HF_BROWSE_ALLOW_STRANGERS and HF_BROWSE_SHOW_EDITED_STATS turn the player's setting on for this session only, never saved (lobby_id NULL); HF_BROWSE_TURN_ON_RELAYS turns co-op over Steam relays on, saved by the launcher (lobby_id NULL). HF_BROWSE_SHOWN and HF_BROWSE_HIDDEN stay on this machine (the terminal's camera) and need no backend. While a mod holds the browser (HF_BROWSE_CLAIM), only that mod's camera and setting asks count. A guide to building a browser of one's own: docs/lobby-browser.md.

### SDK 16: game functions and globals by name (hellforge_functions.h, hellforge_globals.h; check hf->size)

- `function`: `uintptr_t (HfMod* mod, const char* name)`. A game function mappings/functions.json names: its address, or 0 (the mod's log says why: not a mapped name, another game build, before HF_EVENT_GAME_LOADED, or its code is not the reviewed one). Use hf_fn_<Name>(mod), which returns it typed.
- `hook_function`: `int (HfMod* mod, const char* name, void* detour, void** original, int32_t priority)`. hook_ex on a mapped function, by name: 0 when its hook is in, or will go in once the game's code is decoded and reads as reviewed (*original stays NULL until then; the mod's log says when it went in, or why not); 1 refused (not a mapped name, a function only for calling, another game build, no detour or original). Callable from hellforge_mod_init on. Use HF_HOOK(mod, <Name>, detour, &original) or hf_hook_<Name>, which check the types.
- `global`: `uintptr_t (HfMod* mod, const char* name)`. The cell of a game global mappings/globals.json names (it holds a pointer to the game's object): its address, or 0 (the mod's log says why: not a mapped name, another game build, before HF_EVENT_GAME_LOADED, or the code that witnesses it is not the reviewed one). Use hf_global_<Name>(mod), which reads the cell and returns the object typed.

### SDK 16: the mod's status (check hf->size before calling)

- `set_status`: `void (HfMod* mod, const char* text)`. The mod's status for the player: one line (UTF-8, at most 191 bytes; tabs and line breaks read as spaces) of what it does or why it stopped ("waiting for a mission", "off: this game build is not supported"). The launcher's Mods page shows it on the mod's row during the session and after it, and the mod's log records each change. NULL or "" clears it. Set it when it changes: the same text again is only a compare, a new one writes a small file. Any thread. Check hf->size before calling.

### SDK 16: the mod channel (co-op; docs/modding/concepts.md "Co-op")

- `is_authority`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint32_t entity)`. 1 when this machine simulates the entity (its record's authority bit, as HfSpawnEvent.authority says at spawn), 0 not, -1 no such entity. Check hf->size.
- `entity_of_network`: `uint32_t (HfMod* mod, uint32_t network)`. The entity a network id names on this machine now, or 0xFFFFFFFF (game.dll 0xFD9BA0, as abilities.c calls it). Check hf->size.
- `send`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint64_t to, const void* data, uint32_t bytes)`. Messages: the same mod on other machines of the lobby gets them (HF_ON_MESSAGE), in order per sender, reliably while both stay in the lobby; none in solo. to: HF_TO_ALL (every other machine), HF_TO_HOST, or a peer id from an HF_ON_MESSAGE. At most HF_MESSAGE_MAX (1024) bytes; at most 64 messages a second per mod (more: 1 refused, the log says why). 0 sent (queued), 1 refused. Check hf->size.
- `share_set`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint64_t key, const void* value, uint32_t bytes)`. The shared table: rows the HOST sets, which every machine of the lobby holds (a late joiner gets them all when it joins). Per mod: keys are the mod's own (u64), values up to HF_SHARE_VALUE_MAX (64) bytes, at most HF_SHARE_ROWS (512) rows. share_set on the host: bytes 0 removes the row; 0 done, 1 refused (not the host, too big, too many rows: the log says why). Joined machines' share_set is refused. Rows last until the host removes them or the lobby ends. Check hf->size.
- `share_get`: `uint32_t (HfMod* mod, uint64_t key, void* value, uint32_t capacity)`. The row now on this machine: its size (0 none), up to capacity bytes copied. Check hf->size.
- `share_row`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint32_t index, uint64_t* key, void* value, uint32_t capacity, uint32_t* bytes)`. The index-th row this machine holds (any order): 1 with *key and the value filled, 0 past the last. Check hf->size.

### SDK 16: live mods (see the top of this file; check hf->size before calling)

- `load_phase`: `uint32_t (HfMod* mod)`. How the mod was set up: HF_LOAD_BOOT, or HF_LOAD_LIVE while the game runs.
- `unhook`: `int (HfMod* mod, uintptr_t target)`. Takes the mod's own hook of target out of its chain (the function runs as if it had not been hooked by this mod; a call already inside the detour finishes). 0 on success, 1 when the mod has no hook there.

### SDK 17: explosions (check hf->size before calling)

- `add_explosion`: `uint32_t (HfMod* mod, const HfExplosion* explosion)`. Adds a new explosion type (see HfExplosion). Call it from hellforge_mod_init, before the event below. Returns its type (423 and up), or 0 (the mod's log says why). 512 across all mods.
- `explosion_row`: `uintptr_t (uint32_t type)`. The 152-byte ExplosionSettings row of a type a mod added, after HF_EVENT_PROJECTILES_READY, or 0: +0 is the type and must stay.

### SDK 17: beams and arcs (check hf->size before calling)

- `add_beam`: `uint32_t (HfMod* mod, const HfBeam* beam)`. Adds a new beam type (see HfBeam). Call it from hellforge_mod_init. Returns its type (31 and up), or 0 (the mod's log says why). 512 across all mods.
- `beam_row`: `uintptr_t (uint32_t type)`. The 112-byte BeamSettings row of a type a mod added, after HF_EVENT_PROJECTILES_READY, or 0: +0 is the type and must stay.
- `add_arc`: `uint32_t (HfMod* mod, const HfArc* arc)`. Adds a new arc type (see HfArc). Call it from hellforge_mod_init. Returns its type (17 and up), or 0 (the mod's log says why). 512 across all mods.
- `arc_row`: `uintptr_t (uint32_t type)`. The 104-byte ArcSettings row of a type a mod added, after HF_EVENT_PROJECTILES_READY, or 0: +0 is the type and must stay.
- `add_attachment`: `uint32_t (HfMod* mod, const HfAttachment* attachment)`. Adds a weapon attachment (see HfAttachment). Call it from hellforge_mod_init, after add_weapon for its weapons. Returns its item id, or 0 (the mod's log says why). 128 across all mods. Customization screens list it on its weapons once the game has loaded its items; the session's backend makes it the player's.

### SDK 17: enemies (check hf->size before calling)

- `add_enemy`: `int (HfMod* mod, const HfEnemy* enemy)`. Adds an enemy to the spawn director's rosters (see HfEnemy). Call it from hellforge_mod_init. 0 added (its rows are made once the entity data loads, and the log counts them), 1 refused (the mod's log says why).
- `add_enemy_swap`: `int (HfMod* mod, const HfEnemySwap* swap)`. Adds a replacement rule (see HfEnemySwap). Call it from hellforge_mod_init. 0 added, 1 refused (the log says why).
- `add_enemy_squad`: `int (HfMod* mod, const HfEnemySquad* squad)`. Adds a squad (see HfEnemySquad). Call it from hellforge_mod_init. 0 added (its rows are made with the enemies'), 1 refused (the mod's log says why).

### SDK 17: the game's RPCs (hellforge_rpcs.h; docs/modding/networking.md; check hf->size before calling)

- `rpc_listen`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint32_t rpc, HfRpcListener listener, void* user, int32_t priority)`. Listens to an RPC (its HF_RPC_... id, or HF_RPC_ALL for every one) as this machine receives it: on the game thread, before the game's own receiver, highest priority first (equal priorities in the order they listened). A listener that returns HF_RPC_DROP stops the message: later listeners and the game don't see it. Call it from hellforge_mod_init on. 0 listening, 1 refused (an RPC this build doesn't have; the mod's log says why).
- `rpc_send`: `int (HfMod* mod, uint32_t rpc, uint64_t to, const HfRpcArg* args, uint32_t count)`. Sends one of the game's RPCs (hellforge_rpcs.h's hf_rpc_<name>_send builds the arguments): to HF_TO_ALL (every other machine of the session), HF_TO_HOST, or a peer (an HfRpcEvent's sender). The game encodes each argument by its declared type, so values outside a type's range are clamped or wrap. Game thread only. 0 sent, 1 refused (another thread, solo, an unknown RPC, arguments that are not its declared ones, HF_TO_HOST on the host; the mod's log says why).
- `add_screen`: `int (HfMod* mod, const HfScreen* screen)`. SDK 17: screens drawn over the game (hellforge_overlay.h, docs/modding/overlay.md). Adds one, from any thread: its id (1 and up), or 0 refused (modloader.log says why: its key is another screen's, say).
- `screen_show`: `void (HfMod* mod, int id, int open)`. Opens (1) or closes (0) one of the mod's screens, from any thread. Closing sets input->closing; draw is called until it returns 0.

## Functions

Game functions by name (`hellforge_functions.h`, SDK 16), from `mappings/functions.json`. `hf_fn_<Name>(mod)` gives
the function to call; `HF_HOOK(mod, <Name>, detour, &original)` and `hf_hook_<Name>` hook it, for the functions
marked "hook or call" (their whole signature is known). The compiler checks the detour's type against the
signature. Each entry gives where the function is in this build, its signature and its arguments.

See also: [Hooks and game memory](modding/hooks.md#hooking-by-name).

### ConsumeDamageEvent

`game.dll+0x12A7D40`, hook or call. Takes one damage event from this machine's queue in the frame's drain, before ApplyDamage's armour and multipliers. HF_ON_DAMAGE and HF_ON_DAMAGE_PRE come from it; a detour that changes the amount at +0x2C changes the damage. From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research event-combat; HellForge's HF_ON_DAMAGE hook (event_sources.c).

```c
typedef uint64_t (*HfFn_ConsumeDamageEvent)(void* system, uint8_t* event, uint64_t r8, uint64_t r9, uint64_t s5, uint64_t s6);
```

| Argument | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `system` | `void*` | the damage system |
| `event` | `uint8_t*` | the DamageEvent: +0 target entity, +4 kind, +8 element, +0x10 dealer and +0x14 owner network ids, +0x18 creditor peer (u64), +0x2C amount (i32), +0x50 the hit position (3 floats) |
| `r8` | `uint64_t` | not reviewed: pass it on |
| `r9` | `uint64_t` | not reviewed: pass it on |
| `s5` | `uint64_t` | not reviewed: pass it on |
| `s6` | `uint64_t` | not reviewed: pass it on |

### KillDispatch

`game.dll+0xB96310`, hook or call. Tells the game something was killed, on the victim's owner. HF_ON_KILL comes from it. From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research event-combat; HellForge's HF_ON_KILL hook (event_sources.c).

```c
typedef uint64_t (*HfFn_KillDispatch)(void* context, uint64_t creditor, uint32_t victim, uint32_t dealer, uint32_t kind, uint32_t element, uint64_t s7, uint64_t s8);
```

| Argument | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `context` | `void*` | the kill context |
| `creditor` | `uint64_t` | the peer id the kill is credited to |
| `victim` | `uint32_t` | the entity killed |
| `dealer` | `uint32_t` | the entity that dealt the killing damage |
| `kind` | `uint32_t` | the damage kind (HfDamageEvent.kind) |
| `element` | `uint32_t` | the damage element |
| `s7` | `uint64_t` | not reviewed: pass it on |
| `s8` | `uint64_t` | not reviewed: pass it on |

### ActivateStratagemBeacon

`game.dll+0x6ABDB0`, hook or call. Activates a stratagem's beacon once it lands, once per beacon, on the thrower's machine. HF_ON_STRATAGEM comes from it. From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research event-actions; HellForge's HF_ON_STRATAGEM hook (event_sources.c).

```c
typedef uint64_t (*HfFn_ActivateStratagemBeacon)(void* manager, int32_t element, uint32_t thrower, const float* position, void* record, uint32_t type, uint64_t s7, uint64_t s8, uint64_t s9, uint64_t s10);
```

| Argument | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `manager` | `void*` | the stratagem manager |
| `element` | `int32_t` | the beacon's element |
| `thrower` | `uint32_t` | the throwing player's entity |
| `position` | `const float*` | where the beacon landed (3 floats) |
| `record` | `void*` | the beacon's record |
| `type` | `uint32_t` | the StratagemType (HF_STRATAGEM_..., or a mod's from add_stratagem) |
| `s7` | `uint64_t` | not reviewed: pass it on |
| `s8` | `uint64_t` | not reviewed: pass it on |
| `s9` | `uint64_t` | not reviewed: pass it on |
| `s10` | `uint64_t` | not reviewed: pass it on |

### SpawnEntity

`game.dll+0xFDC140`, hook or call. Spawns an entity into this machine's world, remote spawns included. HF_ON_SPAWN comes from it, after the original returns. From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research event-actions; HellForge's HF_ON_SPAWN hook (event_sources.c).

```c
typedef uint64_t (*HfFn_SpawnEntity)(void* manager, uint32_t entity, uint64_t resource, const uint8_t* info, uint64_t s5, uint64_t s6);
```

| Argument | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `manager` | `void*` | the entity manager |
| `entity` | `uint32_t` | the new entity's id in this session |
| `resource` | `uint64_t` | the entity's resource id (HF_ENTITY_..., or a mod's) |
| `info` | `const uint8_t*` | the SpawnInfo: +0 authority byte (1: this machine is in charge of it), +4 network id (u32) |
| `s5` | `uint64_t` | not reviewed: pass it on |
| `s6` | `uint64_t` | not reviewed: pass it on |

### RequestExplosion

`game.dll+0x13C0A80`, hook or call. Requests an explosion in this machine's world: projectile impacts, explosives, stratagems, chained blasts. HF_ON_EXPLOSION comes from it; hf->spawn_explosion is the checked way to call it. From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research event-explosions; HellForge's HF_ON_EXPLOSION hook (event_sources.c).

```c
typedef uint64_t (*HfFn_RequestExplosion)(void* queue, const float* position, uint32_t type, uint32_t source, uint32_t owner, uint64_t creditor, uint64_t a7, uint64_t a8, uint64_t a9, uint64_t a10, uint64_t a11, uint64_t a12, uint64_t a13, uint64_t a14, uint64_t a15);
```

| Argument | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `queue` | `void*` | the explosion queue: hf_global_ExplosionQueue (256 requests a frame) |
| `position` | `const float*` | where (3 floats) |
| `type` | `uint32_t` | the ExplosionSettings row |
| `source` | `uint32_t` | the entity it comes from |
| `owner` | `uint32_t` | the entity that owns it |
| `creditor` | `uint64_t` | the peer id its kills are credited to |
| `a7` | `uint64_t` | the game's callers pass 0 |
| `a8` | `uint64_t` | the game's callers pass NULL |
| `a9` | `uint64_t` | the game's callers pass 1 |
| `a10` | `uint64_t` | the game's callers pass 0 |
| `a11` | `uint64_t` | the game's callers pass NULL |
| `a12` | `uint64_t` | the game's callers pass NULL |
| `a13` | `uint64_t` | the game's callers pass NULL |
| `a14` | `uint64_t` | the game's callers pass 0 |
| `a15` | `uint64_t` | the game's callers pass 0 |

`game.dll+0x129F170`, call only. Queues a status buildup on an entity (4096 a frame: hf_global_CombatQueues' status_count). Host only. hf->apply_status is the checked way to call it. From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research event-combat; HellForge's apply_status (actions.c).

### QueueDamage

`game.dll+0x129F910`, call only. Queues a damage request (4096 a frame: hf_global_CombatQueues' damage_count) that the frame's drain applies. hf->injure is the checked way to call it, on a limb of this machine's Helldiver. From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research event-combat, player-injury-path; HellForge's injure (actions.c).

```c
typedef void (*HfFn_QueueDamage)(void* unused, uint32_t kind, uint32_t a3, uint32_t target, uint32_t amount, uint8_t a6, uint32_t network, uint32_t network2, uint64_t creditor, uint32_t a10, float f11, float f12, float f13, const float* direction, const float* position, uint32_t actor, uint32_t a17, uint32_t a18, float scale);
```

| Argument | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `unused` | `void*` | NULL |
| `kind` | `uint32_t` | the damage kind (HfDamageEvent.kind; injure passes 6, ability, as the VG-70's own self-damage does) |
| `a3` | `uint32_t` | injure passes 0 |
| `target` | `uint32_t` | the entity damaged |
| `amount` | `uint32_t` | the damage |
| `a6` | `uint8_t` | injure passes 1 |
| `network` | `uint32_t` | a network id: injure passes the Helldiver's own |
| `network2` | `uint32_t` | a network id: injure passes the Helldiver's own |
| `creditor` | `uint64_t` | the peer id credited: injure passes the one in the Helldiver's health record (+0x38) |
| `a10` | `uint32_t` | injure passes 0 |
| `f11` | `float` | injure passes 0 |
| `f12` | `float` | injure passes 0 |
| `f13` | `float` | injure passes 0 |
| `direction` | `const float*` | the hit's direction (3 floats; injure passes 0, 0, -1) |
| `position` | `const float*` | the hit's position (3 floats; injure passes 0, 0, 0) |
| `actor` | `uint32_t` | the hit actor: injure passes the limb's, from the engine's UnitActorByName (helldivers2.exe 0x799DE0) |
| `a17` | `uint32_t` | injure passes 9 |
| `a18` | `uint32_t` | injure passes 0 |
| `scale` | `float` | injure passes 1.0 |

`game.dll+0xA431D0`, call only. Starts an action on an entity if it is free to: 1 started, 0 not. hf->resupply_from_pack is the checked way to call it. From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research player-equipment; HellForge's resupply_from_pack (actions.c).

### RequestEntitySpawn

`game.dll+0xFD9710`, call only. The entity spawn request: makes an entity of a resource at a pose; returns out. hf->spawn_pelican is the checked way to call it (host only, in a mission, with shuttle_transport). From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research pelican; HellForge's spawn_pelican (actions.c).

```c
typedef uint32_t* (*HfFn_RequestEntitySpawn)(uint32_t* out, uint64_t resource, void* spawn);
```

| Argument | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `out` | `uint32_t*` | receives the new entity's id (0: none made) |
| `resource` | `uint64_t` | the entity's resource id (HF_ENTITY_...), registered in this mission |
| `spawn` | `void*` | 0x58 bytes: +0 1, +4 network id (0x7FFF none), +8 the pose (4x4 floats, the position in the last row), +0x48 a pointer to a 0x8A0-byte spawn context (a transport Pelican's hover point at +0x610) |

### AbilityTick

`game.dll+0x11509E0`, call only. Runs one tick of an ability, switching on its id through a 2867-entry table: 1 when it is done, 0 while it runs (and for an unknown id). HellForge points its id check at a stub for abilities of its own (hf->add_ability). From HellForge's abilities (abilities.c).

```c
typedef uint8_t (*HfFn_AbilityTick)(void* ctx, uint32_t id, uint32_t tick);
```

| Argument | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `ctx` | `void*` | the ability's context: +8 the entity it runs on |
| `id` | `uint32_t` | the ability (1 .. 2867; HellForge's own from 2868) |
| `tick` | `uint32_t` | the tick, 60 a second from 0 |

### CollectOperationsForDifficulty

`game.dll+0x11E4CD0`, hook or call. Collects the operation settings rows (25 of 0x490, lowest and highest difficulty at +0xC / +0x10) that allow a difficulty and match the caller's kind, and returns how many: the war table's mission board only offers a level they allow, so HellForge lets every row allowing 10 allow 11. From HellForge's level 11 (difficulty.c DIF_OPERATIONS).

```c
typedef uint32_t (*HfFn_CollectOperationsForDifficulty)(uint64_t unused, uint32_t rdx, uint32_t r8, uint32_t difficulty, uint32_t s5, uint32_t s6, uint64_t unused2, uint32_t* rows);
```

| Argument | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `unused` | `uint64_t` | not read (callers leave the value they store in s5) |
| `rdx` | `uint32_t` | not reviewed: pass it on (callers pass a u16 of their descriptor, +0x10; it goes on to 0x12DF110) |
| `r8` | `uint32_t` | not reviewed: pass it on (-1 or a looked-up id; it goes on to 0x12DF110) |
| `difficulty` | `uint32_t` | the difficulty level (callers pass a byte, their descriptor's +0x20) |
| `s5` | `uint32_t` | not reviewed: must equal the operation row's u32 before its highest difficulty (6 adds an account check) |
| `s6` | `uint32_t` | not reviewed: must equal the operation row's u32 4 bytes below its lowest difficulty |
| `unused2` | `uint64_t` | not read |
| `rows` | `uint32_t*` | receives the index (0..24) of each matching operation row |

| `r9` | `void*` | not reviewed: pass it on (a second {u32, u32} list, cleared and filled by the weld) |

### SetupPhysicsConfig

`game.dll+0x4EE160`, hook or call. game.dll's plugin setup_game, once per process (from its plugin table, no direct caller): takes the engine's APIs, then fills the engine's physics config with its pool sizes, from which the exe builds every world's pools. HellForge raises those sizes before it runs. From HellForge's physics pools (difficulty.c).


| Argument | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `get_api` | `void*` | the engine's API getter, (u32 id) -> API table; it asks for ids 0x28, 0x1F, 6 and more |

### SiloRemoteLink

`game.dll+0x76B580`, hook or call. A silo remote's link, which its laser update runs every frame: while the remote's linker record says a silo link (+4 0x06212002) and the missile is not launched yet, it launches it (engine, explosive set off), names the explosive's owner, sets its guidance target to the laser's point and has the rack let it go. HellForge's silo countdown repeats these steps in this order. From HellForge's silo countdown (abilities.c kLaunchFunctions).

```c
typedef void (*HfFn_SiloRemoteLink)(uint64_t unused, uint32_t remote);
```

| Argument | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `unused` | `uint64_t` | not read (its caller passes hf_global_WeaponLinkerManager's cell) |
| `remote` | `uint32_t` | the targeting remote's entity |

### LaserDesignatorUpdate

`game.dll+0x9BF6D0`, hook or call. Updates every laser designator for a frame (a silo remote's laser) and calls each remote's link (SiloRemoteLink), which launches the missile on the first frame the laser marks a point. From HellForge's silo countdown (abilities.c).

```c
typedef void (*HfFn_LaserDesignatorUpdate)(void* designators);
```

| Argument | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `designators` | `void*` | the laser designators (the world system passes its + 0x75C2B8) |

Reached through a component callback thunk (0x528870). From HellForge's silo countdown (abilities.c).

### RealizePellet

`game.dll+0x615940`, hook or call. Emits one pellet of a ProjectileWeapon shot: builds its descriptor and spawns it into the projectile pool (SpawnProjectile, at 0x616612), on every machine. HellForge swaps the programmable round per player at 0x615B23 and gives other players' shots their own rows at the pool call. From HellForge's per-player shots (shots.c); research projectile-per-player (ProjectileWeapon_RealizePellet).


| `transform` | `const uint8_t*` | the shot's transform: +0x10 its direction, +0x30 its position |
| `owner` | `uint32_t` | the owner or wielder entity (its creditor comes from Creditor) |
| `s6` | `float` | not reviewed: pass it on (copied into the pool's options) |
| `s7` | `uint32_t` | not reviewed: pass it on (a shot random or token) |
| `s8` | `uint8_t` | not reviewed: pass it on |
| `s9` | `float` | not reviewed: pass it on |
| `s10` | `float` | not reviewed: pass it on |
| `s11` | `float` | not reviewed: pass it on |
| `s12` | `uint8_t` | not reviewed: pass it on (selects the ProjectileWeapon +0x108 option) |

### WeaponHeatInstanceStep

`game.dll+0x762F60`, hook or call. One weapon's heat for a frame, on its owner: charging adds heat, else it cools at +128 per second (overheated or charging: +140, none when +144 is set) times the climate multiplier; heat at the capacity +96 sets the overheated flag when +80 is set, heat down to +100 clears it. The heat (live row +0x58, 12 bytes: +4 heat, +8 overheated) goes out as network field 0xA5023836. From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research overheat-cooling (heatStep 0x762F60), reload-heat-knife; WeaponHeatUpdate's call 0x7649CB.

```c
typedef void (*HfFn_WeaponHeatInstanceStep)(void* heats, uint32_t index, float dt);
```

| Argument | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `heats` | `void*` | WeaponHeats |
| `index` | `uint32_t` | the instance index |
| `dt` | `float` | the frame time (xmm2, read after the leaf WeaponHeatRecord call) |

### ChargeMultiplier

`game.dll+0x73E280`, hook or call. A charged shot's multiplier from the weapon's charge time (live row +0x40, 40 bytes, +8): below full charge (+0x18) from at_min to 1, above it from 1 to at_overcharge. ### WeaponRefill

`game.dll+0x757F70`, hook or call. Refills one weapon from a resupply: MagazineRefill for a magazine weapon, else RoundsRefill for one loaded by rounds, else HeatsinkRefill. resupply_all calls it for every carried weapon. From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research team-reload-ammo (0x880E20 resupply_all through 0x757F70).

```c
typedef void (*HfFn_WeaponRefill)(uint64_t unused, uint32_t weapon, float scale);
```

|---|---|---|
| `unused` | `uint64_t` | not read |
| `weapon` | `uint32_t` | the weapon entity |
| `scale` | `float` | the share of a resupply's amount |

### MagazineRefill

`game.dll+0x76E6D0`, hook or call. Gives a weapon spare magazines from a resupply: min(spare + max(1, magazines_from_supply x scale), the maximum), sent as network field 0xEC64918B; on the weapon's owner. From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research team-reload-ammo (magazines 0x76E6D0, write at 0x76E7E1).

```c
typedef void (*HfFn_MagazineRefill)(uint64_t unused, uint32_t weapon, float scale);
```

| `weapon` | `uint32_t` | the weapon entity |
| `scale` | `float` | the share of a resupply's amount |

### SpawnSquad

`game.dll+0x953580`, hook or call. Spawns a squad of enemies from a composition at a position: the director's patrols (EnemyDirectorTick), spawners (AiSpawnerUpdate), bug holes (BugholeUpdate), dropships (SquadTransportUpdate) and outposts (FillOutpost) all spawn through it. Returns the squad, or NULL. From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research spawn-channels-dev10 (selected patrol creation); game.dll: its six callers' stack arguments (+0x20 to +0x48) and the return they test.

```c
typedef void* (*HfFn_SpawnSquad)(void* hivemind, void* composition, const float* position, uint32_t r9, uint32_t flags, uint64_t s6, uint64_t s7, uint64_t s8, float s9, uint32_t s10);
```

| Argument | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `hivemind` | `void*` | the HiveMind manager: hf_global_HiveMindManager |
| `composition` | `void*` | the composition to spawn, as the pickers return it |

### MurmurHash64A

```c
typedef int32_t (*HfFn_UnitCrossfadeAnimation)(uint32_t unit, uint64_t animation, int32_t layer, float blend_time, uint32_t s5, uint32_t s6);
```

| Argument | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `unit` | `uint32_t` | the unit's handle |
| `animation` | `uint64_t` | the animation's IdString64 |
| `layer` | `int32_t` | the layer (the binding takes it 1-based; 0 when absent) |
| `blend_time` | `float` | the crossfade time in seconds (0 when absent) |
| `s5` | `uint32_t` | loop: 1 loops (Lua's 5th argument, default 1) |
| `s6` | `uint32_t` | blend type: 1 when Lua's 6th argument is "offset", 0 for "normal" or absent |

### SpawnEntityAddComponents

`game.dll+0x581320`, hook or call. SpawnEntity's first component pass: for each of the new entity's components, its add callback with the spawn data (kind 1), then its setup callback with a scratch buffer (kind 2). Every machine runs it for every spawn, remote ones included; the entity is not in the network id map yet. From HellForge research (build 25480438): SpawnEntity's call at 0xFDC25A, the callback table filled at 0x551B60.

```c
typedef void (*HfFn_SpawnEntityAddComponents)(void* components, uint8_t* record, const uint8_t* info, void* ids);
```

| Argument | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `components` | `void*` | the entity manager's component callbacks (EntityManager + 0x40): one array of 324 function pointers per callback kind, indexed by component type |
| `record` | `uint8_t*` | the new entity's world record (+0 the entity, u64; +0x10 its network id, u32; +0x14 its flags) |
| `info` | `const uint8_t*` | the SpawnInfo SpawnEntity was given: +0 authority byte (1: this machine is in charge of it) |
| `ids` | `void*` | the object at EntityManager + 0x8: the spawned entity's id goes into its table at +0xB020 |

### SpawnEntityFinishComponents

`game.dll+0x581780`, hook or call. SpawnEntity's last component pass: each of the new entity's components' finish callback (kind 3), after every component exists and the entity is in the network id map. A detour that runs after the original sees the entity fully set up, before SpawnEntity returns. From HellForge research (build 25480438): SpawnEntity's call at 0xFDC2B1, the callback table filled at 0x551B60.

```c
typedef void (*HfFn_SpawnEntityFinishComponents)(void* components, uint8_t* record, const uint8_t* info);
```

| Argument | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `components` | `void*` | the entity manager's component callbacks (EntityManager + 0x40) |
| `record` | `uint8_t*` | the new entity's world record |
| `info` | `const uint8_t*` | the SpawnInfo SpawnEntity was given |

### ActivateEntity

`game.dll+0xFDBB40`, hook or call. Switches an entity on in this machine's world: finds it by network id, sets bit 0 of its record's flags (+0x14) and runs each of its components' activate callbacks (kinds 6, then 7), unless a tag or a session flag holds it back. The session calls it through LocalUser's entity callbacks (+0xB3D0, set at 0x134D470, via a thunk at 0xBA0670). From HellForge research (build 25480438): 0xFDBB40, its thunks at 0xBA0670 and 0xFDA130, the callback table filled at 0x551B60.

```c
typedef void (*HfFn_ActivateEntity)(void* unused, uint32_t entity, uint64_t ctx, uint64_t r9);
```

| Argument | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `unused` | `void*` | not read |
| `entity` | `uint32_t` | the entity's network id |
| `ctx` | `uint64_t` | the caller's second argument (through the thunk), passed on unchanged to each component's activate callbacks |
| `r9` | `uint64_t` | not reviewed: pass it on |

### DeactivateEntity

`game.dll+0xFDBEA0`, hook or call. Switches an entity off in this machine's world: finds it by network id and, if it is on (bit 0 of its record's flags, +0x14), runs each of its components' deactivate callbacks (kinds 9, then 8) and clears the bit. The entity stays in the world. The session calls it through LocalUser's entity callbacks (+0xB3D8, set at 0x134D470, via a thunk at 0xBA0680). From HellForge research (build 25480438): 0xFDBEA0, its thunks at 0xBA0680 and 0xFDA140, 0x581E10.

```c
typedef void (*HfFn_DeactivateEntity)(void* unused, uint32_t entity, uint64_t ctx);
```

| Argument | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `unused` | `void*` | not read |
| `entity` | `uint32_t` | the entity's network id |
| `ctx` | `uint64_t` | the caller's second argument (through the thunk), passed on unchanged to each component's deactivate callbacks |

## Globals

The game's objects by name (`hellforge_globals.h`, SDK 16), from `mappings/globals.json`: its managers and queues,
what the functions above take. `hf_global_<Name>(mod)` gives the object as an `Hf<Name>*`, or NULL. Each entry gives
where its cell is in this build, what is known of the object, and its named fields.

See also: [Hooks and game memory](modding/hooks.md#game-globals).

### Game

`game.dll+0x3326340` (a pointer to it), `HfGame`. The Game: the session's top object, whose state the game's ChangeState (game.dll+0xAB2FA0) sets. From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research event-state; HellForge's HF_ON_STATE poll (event_sources.c).

| Field | Offset | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `wwise_world` | +0x10F8 | ptr | the WwiseWorld: +0x4148 its default source (u32), heard everywhere |
| `state` | +0xAC21C | u32 | the game's state (HF_STATE_...; hf->game_state reads it) |
| `level` | +0xAE288 | ptr | the mission's level (the level generator, lg): +0xF9 its difficulty level (u8), +0x2C8 its generation block, +0x2D70 the playable radius / 1024 (f32) |

### PlayerManager

`game.dll+0x3326468` (a pointer to it), `HfPlayerManager`. The players of the session. Per slot i (0 to count - 1): the peer id at +0x2C8 + 0x38 i (u64), its lifecycle at +0x2E0 + 0x38 i (u32: 2 waiting to respawn, 3 spawned), its player entity at [+0xE8 + 8 i] + 8 (u32), its Helldiver's network id at +0x3A8 + 0x20 i. Slots move as players leave: tell players apart by peer id. From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research event-mission; HellForge's HF_ON_PLAYER_SPAWN poll (event_sources.c).

| Field | Offset | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `count` | +0x84 | u32 | players in the session (at most 4) |

### LocalUser

`game.dll+0x347CEF0` (a pointer to it), `HfLocalUser`. This machine's user. From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research event-mission; HellForge's local player (event_sources.c).

| Field | Offset | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `peer` | +0xB398 | u64 | this machine's player's peer id (HfPlayerEvent.peer) |
| `host_peer` | +0xB3A8 | u64 | the game's host's peer id (it changes with a host migration) |

### GameMode

`game.dll+0x33266A0` (a pointer to it), `HfGameMode`. The game mode. From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research event-actions; HellForge's actions (actions.c ActMission).

| Field | Offset | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `mode` | +0x8 | u32 | 1 in a mission |
| `mission` | +0x38 | ptr | the mission's data: +0x14 bit 0 set on the host, +8 the game mode's entity (u32) |

### EntityManager

`game.dll+0x346BF98` (a pointer to it), `HfEntityManager`. The entities of this machine's world. At +0xF22EC8 a hash map of network ids to slots (8-byte keys, 4-byte values), and at +0xF32F20 the network objects by slot, 24 bytes each (+0 the entity id, u32); at +0xF1AEB0 the map of entity ids, at +0xF32F18 their descriptors. A mod may move the descriptors, the network objects with them (they are one record): docs/modding/hooks.md "Fields a mod moved". From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research event-combat; HellForge's event polls (event_polls.c).

| Field | Offset | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `component_tables` | +0xF12478 | ptr | component table 0; table c's pointer at +8 c: rows {u64 entity, u32 record, u32 0} from entity % slots, then the records |
| `entity_deltas` | +0xF12E98 | ptr | the entity deltas a weapon's chosen attachments key into (by their add_path); reloaded at every use |
| `entity_map` | +0xF12EA0 | ptr | the entity map: 4096 rows of {u64 entity, u16* components, u64 component count, u32 short id, u32 0}, from entity % 4096 |
| `short_ids` | +0xF12EA8 | ptr | the short ids: sorted {u32 short id, u32 map row}; an entity's index here is its type in the engine |

### HealthManager

`game.dll+0x3326688` (a pointer to it), `HfHealthManager`. Every entity's health. At +0x1030 a hash map of entity ids to indexes (8-byte keys, 4-byte values); an index's descriptor names its entity (+0 resource u64, +8 entity u32, +12 unit, +16 network id, +20 bit 0 owned here); its record holds health (i32) at +0x14 and its life (u32: 0 alive, 1 downed, 2 or more dead) at +0x19C; its ext record the maximum health (i32) at +0x14. From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research event-combat; HellForge's HF_ON_HEAL poll (event_polls.c) and heal (actions.c).

| Field | Offset | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `count` | +0x1020 | u32 | indexes in use |
| `descriptors` | +0x1048 | ptr | an array of descriptor pointers, by index |
| `records` | +0x1058 | ptr | the health records, 0x1B8 bytes each, by index |
| `ext` | +0x1060 | ptr | the ext records, 0x1C bytes each, by index |

### MotionManager

`game.dll+0x3326558` (a pointer to it), `HfMotionManager`. Entities that move. At +0x48A0 a hash map of entity ids to indexes (8-byte keys, 4-byte values); an index's descriptor names its entity at +8 (u32) and has bit 0 of +0x14 set while it is live; its record starts with its velocity (3 floats, m/s). From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research player-avatar-actions; HellForge's add_velocity (actions.c).

| Field | Offset | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `count` | +0x4880 | u32 | indexes in use |
| `descriptors` | +0x48B8 | ptr | an array of descriptor pointers, by index |
| `records` | +0x48C8 | ptr | the motion records, 0x84 bytes each, by index |
| `positions` | +0x48D0 | ptr | 0xA4 bytes per index: the position (3 floats) MoveObject writes |
| `sources` | +0x48D8 | ptr | 0x2C bytes per index: the position motion starts from each frame (3 floats; relative for an object riding a parent, the record's +0x3C not -1) |

| Field | Offset | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `map` | +0x30 | ptr | the entity map's entries, 8 bytes each (entity u32, index u32); the map's capacity (u32) is 8 bytes after this field, its empty key 0xC after it, its hash multiplier 0x10 after it |

### TurretManager

`game.dll+0x3326D70` (a pointer to it), `HfTurretManager`. The TurretComponent manager (component world +0xEB5060): Turrets (sentries, mounted guns: TurretUpdate's first argument; TurretSettings resolves their records, private copies at +0xA8). At +0x28 a map of entities to indexes. From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research sentry-components and vehicle-mech-components (turret manager game+0x3326D70); game.dll: TurretComponent's growth function 0x6E1120 names it and its add helpers load this cell; the world's constructor (0x568230) stores component world +0xEB5060 here.

| Field | Offset | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `map` | +0x28 | ptr | the entity map's entries, 8 bytes each (entity u32, index u32); the map's capacity (u32) is 8 bytes after this field, its empty key 0xC after it, its hash multiplier 0x10 after it |

### LocomotionManager

`game.dll+0x3326CF8` (a pointer to it), `HfLocomotionManager`. The LocomotionComponent manager (component world +0xF06698): Locomotion (how characters move; private copies at +0xA8, copy map at +0x68). At +0x20 a map of entities to indexes. From HD2Runtime 0.40.1 research vehicle-mech-components (lifecycle.managers: LocomotionComponentData); game.dll: LocomotionComponent's growth function 0x9CE510 names it and its add helpers load this cell; the world's constructor (0x568230) stores component world +0xF06698 here.

| Field | Offset | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|
| `map` | +0x20 | ptr | the entity map's entries, 8 bytes each (entity u32, index u32); the map's capacity (u32) is 8 bytes after this field, its empty key 0xC after it, its hash multiplier 0x10 after it |

## Components

Entity component records. In a definition block, `HF_FIELD(Component, field)` names a field; for an entity's own
record, `hf_record(mod, entity, HfComponent)` gives a typed pointer (both in `hellforge_mod.h`). Each entry starts
with the game's name for the component, its component number and its record size, then lists its fields:

- Offset: where the field starts in the record, in bytes.
- Type: `u8`, `u16`, `u32`, `i32`, `f32`, `u64` or `bytes`, with a count in brackets for an array.
- Applies: when a live change takes effect in a running game. `now` is the next time the game reads it, `respawn`
  when the entity is next made (a spawn, a re-equip, the next call-in), `mission` when a mission loads. A `?` means
  inferred, not yet confirmed.
- Meaning: what the field does. "(unverified)" marks a field named from research but not yet confirmed in the game;
  "(a projectile reference)" and the like mark a field whose value is a row of that table.

Each component's layout is the game build its offsets were reviewed on. The loader refuses fields of a layout it
does not have, so a mod built against another layout is reported instead of run.

See also: [Content](modding/content.md) for changing existing content by field name, [Logic at run
time](modding/runtime.md) for live edits, [Stats mods](modding/stats-mods.md) for number changes without code, and
[Debugging](modding/debugging.md) for why a field was refused.

### HfProjectileWeapon

`ProjectileWeaponComponentData`: component 321, 616-byte records. a weapon that fires projectiles: what, how fast, its casings and firing sounds.

| Field | Offset | Type | Applies | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `projectile` | +0x00 | u32 | respawn | the projectile type it fires (HF_PROJECTILE_...) (a projectile reference) |
| `fire_rate` | +0x04 | f32[3] | respawn | rounds per minute in its rate slots; the middle one is the default, seeding the live rate at spawn |
| `zeroing_slots` | +0x10 | f32[3] | respawn | zeroing distances (unverified) |
| `zeroing_hipfire` | +0x1C | f32 | respawn | zeroing from the hip (unverified) |
| `zeroing_slot` | +0x20 | u32 | respawn | the zeroing slot in use (unverified) |
| `infinite_ammo` | +0x24 | u8 | respawn | 1: never runs out (unverified) |
| `projectile_entity` | +0x28 | u64 | respawn | an entity spawned per shot instead of a projectile, or 0 |
| `heat_buildup` | +0x34 | f32 | respawn | heat per shot (unverified) |
| `maximum_heat` | +0x38 | f32 | respawn | most heat (unverified) |
| `heat_bleed_speed` | +0x3C | f32 | respawn | heat lost per second (unverified) |
| `heat_bleed_delay` | +0x40 | f32 | respawn | seconds before heat bleeds (unverified) |
| `speed_multiplier` | +0x7C | f32 | respawn | projectile speed multiplier (unverified) |
| `spinup_time` | +0x90 | f32 | respawn | seconds to spin up (unverified) |
| `rpc_synced_fire_events` | +0x94 | u8 | respawn | 1: only its creator decides when it fires (networked fire) |
| `ejection_effect` | +0xA0 | u64 | respawn | ejection particle effect (unverified) |
| `ejection_node` | +0xA8 | u32 | respawn | the node casings leave from (hash) (unverified) |
| `casing_effect` | +0xB0 | u64 | respawn | the casings' particle effect |
| `casing_node` | +0xB8 | u32[4] | respawn | casing nodes (hashes) (unverified) |
| `link_effect` | +0xC8 | u64 | respawn | belt links' particle effect (unverified) |
| `link_node` | +0xD0 | u32 | respawn | the node belt links leave from (hash) (unverified) |
| `casing_impact_type` | +0xD4 | u32 | respawn | the sound casings make landing |
| `casing_audio_event` | +0xD8 | u32 | respawn | the casings' audio event |
| `casing_playbacks` | +0xDC | u32 | respawn | how many times the casing sound plays |
| `muzzle_flash` | +0xE0 | u64 | respawn | muzzle flash effect (unverified) |
| `shockwave_type` | +0xE8 | u32 | respawn | muzzle shockwave (unverified) |
| `use_faint_shockwave` | +0xEC | u8 | respawn | 1: a faint shockwave (unverified) |
| `use_midi_event_system` | +0xED | u8 | respawn | 1: posts a note per shot and no loop start (the firing sounds of MIDI weapons) |
| `midi_timing_randomization` | +0xF0 | f32[2] | respawn | MIDI timing randomization |
| `midi_stop_delay` | +0xF8 | f32 | respawn | MIDI stop delay (unverified) |
| `fire_loop_start_audio_event` | +0xFC | u32 | respawn | posted when it starts firing |
| `fire_loop_stop_audio_event` | +0x100 | u32 | respawn | posted when it stops firing |
| `fire_single_audio_event` | +0x104 | u32 | respawn | posted every shot |
| `audio_event_108` | +0x108 | u32 | respawn | an audio event of the firing sounds; its name (38 characters) is not known (unverified) |
| `fire_single_audio_event_mode4` | +0x10C | u32 | respawn | posted every shot in fire mode 4, when set |
| `fire_single_audio_event_mode7` | +0x110 | u32 | respawn | posted every shot in fire mode 7, when set |
| `haptics_loop_stop_event` | +0x114 | u32 | respawn | secondary loop-stop event (controller haptics, inferred) (unverified) |
| `haptics_single_event` | +0x118 | u32 | respawn | secondary per-shot event (controller haptics, inferred) (unverified) |
| `fire_loop_camera_shake` | +0x120 | u64 | respawn | camera shake while firing (unverified) |
| `fire_loop_camera_shake_radius` | +0x128 | f32[2] | respawn | its inner and outer radius (unverified) |
| `silenced_fire_loop_start_audio_event` | +0x210 | u32 | respawn | fire_loop_start_audio_event when silenced |
| `silenced_fire_loop_stop_audio_event` | +0x214 | u32 | respawn | fire_loop_stop_audio_event when silenced |
| `silenced_fire_single_audio_event` | +0x218 | u32 | respawn | fire_single_audio_event when silenced |
| `fire_node` | +0x21C | u32 | respawn | the node it fires from (hash of "muzzle") |
| `silenced` | +0x22C | u8 | respawn | 1: posts the silenced events |
| `weapon_function_projectile_type` | +0x240 | u32 | respawn | the projectile type it fires with programmable ammunition on (a projectile reference) |

Group `casings` (the casings it ejects: their effect and sounds): `casing_effect`, `casing_impact_type`, `casing_audio_event`, `casing_playbacks`.

Group `firing_sounds` (every firing sound, silenced or not (HfWeapon.sound_from copies the same)): `use_midi_event_system`, `midi_timing_randomization`, `midi_stop_delay`, `fire_loop_start_audio_event`, `fire_loop_stop_audio_event`, `fire_single_audio_event`, `audio_event_108`, `fire_single_audio_event_mode4`, `fire_single_audio_event_mode7`, `haptics_loop_stop_event`, `haptics_single_event`, `silenced_fire_loop_start_audio_event`, `silenced_fire_loop_stop_audio_event`, `silenced_fire_single_audio_event`, `silenced`.

| `vertical_spread` | +0x58 | f32 | respawn | spread up and down (mrad, full width) |

| `sway` | +0x68 | f32 | respawn | sway multiplier (unverified) |
| `suppressed` | +0x70 | u8 | respawn | 1: suppressed (unverified) |
| `burst_rounds` | +0x8C | u32 | respawn | rounds per burst (unverified) |

| `weapon_function_left` | +0xB8 | u32 | respawn | left weapon function (unverified) |
| `weapon_function_right` | +0xBC | u32 | respawn | right weapon function (unverified) |
| `ergonomics` | +0x164 | f32 | respawn | ergonomics (unverified) |
| `stationary_while_firing` | +0x183 | u8 | respawn | 1: the wielder stands still while firing (unverified) |
| `crosshair_type` | +0x190 | u32 | respawn | crosshair (unverified) |
| `fire_ability` | +0x3AC | u32 | respawn | an ability the weapon runs when it fires (0: none) (unverified) |
| `fire_ability_2` | +0x3B0 | u32 | respawn | a second fire-time ability (the VG-70 Variable: 50, a lead for its knock-down when fired standing; 0 on the AMR and Autocannon) (unverified) |

Group `recoil` (both recoil blocks, sideways and up): `recoil_drift_horizontal`, `recoil_drift_vertical`, `recoil_climb_horizontal`, `recoil_climb_vertical`.

Group `spread` (spread sideways and up and down): `horizontal_spread`, `vertical_spread`.

### HfWeaponMagazine

`WeaponMagazineComponentData`: component 5, 160-byte records. a weapon's magazine: what it loads, how many rounds, spare magazines.

| Field | Offset | Type | Applies | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `magazine_type` | +0x00 | u32 | respawn? | 1: rounds follow pattern (unverified) |
| `pattern` | +0x04 | u32[32] | respawn? | projectile types of successive rounds; with magazine_type 1, round n fires entry (n - 1) % count (game.dll 0x7456D0); never 0 inside the count, which is fixed at spawn (a projectile reference) |
| `first_projectile` | +0x84 | u32 | respawn? | the projectile type chambered first (unverified) |
| `capacity` | +0x88 | u32 | respawn? | rounds per magazine (a turret's live count is an 11-bit network field: at most 2047) |
| `starting_magazines` | +0x8C | u32 | respawn? | spare magazines at the start (unverified) |
| `magazines_from_supply` | +0x90 | u32 | respawn? | spare magazines a resupply gives (unverified) |
| `max_magazines` | +0x94 | u32 | respawn? | most spare magazines carried (unverified) |
| `reload_threshold` | +0x98 | u32 | respawn? | rounds left when it reloads (unverified) |
| `chambered` | +0x9C | u8 | respawn? | 1: a round is chambered on top of the magazine (unverified) |

## Settings tables

Rows of the game's settings tables, found by their id (`set_live`, `set_field`), or through a row function where the
entry names one (`api->...`). The columns are the same as for components; each entry gives the row size instead of
a component number.

See also: [Content](modding/content.md) for settings rows and their row functions, [Logic at run
time](modding/runtime.md) for live edits, and [Stats mods](modding/stats-mods.md) for projectiles, damage, explosions
and statuses without code.

### HfDifficultySettings

`DifficultySettings`: 816-byte rows found by id, `api->difficulty_row`. a difficulty level's settings (levels 1 to 11).

| Field | Offset | Type | Applies | Meaning |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `unlock_item` | +0x00 | u32 | mission | the inventory item that unlocks it (must stay) |
| `level` | +0x04 | u32 | mission | its level (must stay) |
| `name_text` | +0x08 | u32 | mission | text id of its name |
| `description_text` | +0x0C | u32 | mission | text id of its description (unverified) |
| `icon_sprite` | +0x10 | u64 | mission | its icon (an atlas sprite) |
| `optional_objectives` | +0x28 | u32 | mission | optional objectives a mission draws (level 10: 4) |
| `major_objectives` | +0x2C | u32 | mission | secondary objectives (level 10: 1) |
| `outposts` | +0x34 | u32[20] | mission | enemy outposts of each size |
| `encounter_points` | +0xE4 | f32 | now? | spawn budget of patrols, drops and breaches (level 10: 600) |
| `guardforce_points` | +0xE8 | f32 | now? | budget of the map's guards (level 10: 600) |
| `tech_level` | +0xEC | u8 | mission | the enemies' tech level (their spawn weights by level) |
| `map_radius` | +0xF0 | f32[4] | mission | playable radius by map size |
| `spawner_cooldown` | +0x104 | f32 | now? | seconds between the shared spawners' waves (level 10: 1.5) |
| `patrol_interval` | +0x2A0 | f32[3] | now | seconds between roaming patrols: Terminids, Automatons, Illuminate (level 10: 65, 78, 75) |
| `patrol_cap` | +0x2AC | u32[3] | now | most living patrol enemies, per faction (level 10: 41, 39, 48) |

| `push_force` | +0x24 | u32 | now? | push force (unverified) |
| `status_1_type` | +0x2C | u32 | now? | the status effect type it applies in slot 1 (0: empty; slots fill from 1, no gaps) (a status reference) |
| `status_1_strength` | +0x30 | f32 | now? | Status strength (factor) (unverified) |
| `status_2_type` | +0x34 | u32 | now? | the status effect type it applies in slot 2 (0: empty; slots fill from 1, no gaps) (a status reference) |

| `status_4_type` | +0x44 | u32 | now? | the status effect type it applies in slot 4 (0: empty; slots fill from 1, no gaps) (a status reference) |
